## 30. toolResult
import { describe, it, expect, beforeAll, afterAll } from 'vitest';
import * as fs from 'fs';
import * as path from 'path';
import * as os from 'os';
import { analyzeBundlePipeline, cleanupPipelineResult } from '../../../src/pipeline/analyze.js';
import { createTestBundle, getFixturePath } from '../../helpers/create-test-bundle.js';

let tmpDir: string;
let wrongDbBundlePath: string;
let realisticFormatBundlePath: string;

beforeAll(async () => {
  tmpDir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'sbi-real-bundle-'));

  wrongDbBundlePath = await createTestBundle(
    getFixturePath('wrong-db-endpoint'),
    path.join(tmpDir, 'wrong-db-endpoint.tar.gz'),
  );

  realisticFormatBundlePath = await createTestBundle(
    getFixturePath('realistic-bundle-format'),
    path.join(tmpDir, 'realistic-bundle-format.tar.gz'),
  );
});

afterAll(() => {
  fs.rmSync(tmpDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
});

async function runPipeline(bundlePath: string) {
  const result = await analyzeBundlePipeline(bundlePath, { noAi: true });
  afterAll(() => cleanupPipelineResult(result));
  return result;
}

describe('Real bundle regression corpus', () => {
  it('wrong-db-endpoint archive ranks dns-failure above generic connectivity-failure', async () => {
    const result = await runPipeline(wrongDbBundlePath);
    const topHypothesis = result.findings.find((finding) =>
      finding.id.startsWith('finding:hypothesis:'),
    );

    expect(topHypothesis).toBeDefined();
    expect(topHypothesis!.id).toContain('finding:hypothesis:dns-failure:');
  });

  it('wrong-db-endpoint archive does not emit numeric-only service evidence', async () => {
    const result = await runPipeline(wrongDbBundlePath);
    const combinedEvidence = result.findings
      .flatMap((finding) => finding.evidence)
      .map((evidence) => `${evidence.path} ${evidence.note}`)
      .join(' ');

    expect(combinedEvidence).not.toContain('cluster-resources/services/default/5432.yaml');
    expect(combinedEvidence).not.toMatch(/hostname '\s*5432\s*'/i);
    expect(combinedEvidence).not.toMatch(/service '\s*5432\s*'/i);
  });

  it('wrong-db-endpoint top hypothesis reasoning explains why it beat the runner-up', async () => {
    const result = await runPipeline(wrongDbBundlePath);
    const topHypothesis = result.findings.find((finding) =>
      finding.id.startsWith('finding:hypothesis:'),
    );

    expect(topHypothesis).toBeDefined();
    expect(topHypothesis!.reasoningSummary).toContain('Preferred over runner-up');
  });

  it('realistic Troubleshoot-format archive still parses successfully as a regression corpus bundle', async () => {
    const result = await runPipeline(realisticFormatBundlePath);

    expect(result.profile.analyzerCount).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(result.profile.resourceCount).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(result.profile.eventCount).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(result.profile.logFileCount).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });
});

## 31. toolResult
#!/usr/bin/env node
import fs from 'fs/promises';
import path from 'path';
import os from 'os';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
import { spawnSync } from 'child_process';
import * as tar from 'tar';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);
const repoRoot = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
const cliEntry = path.join(repoRoot, 'dist', 'cli', 'run.js');

const SCENARIOS = [
  {
    name: 'wrong-db-endpoint',
    expectedTopCategory: 'dns-failure',
  },
  {
    name: 'missing-secret-crashloop',
    expectedTopCategory: 'missing-secret',
  },
  {
    name: 'bad-image-tag',
    expectedTopCategory: 'image-pull-failure',
  },
  {
    name: 'pvc-pending',
    expectedTopCategory: 'volume-mount-failure',
  },
];

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Category inference
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Infers the diagnostic category of a single finding from its ID or text.
 *
 * This mirrors the heuristic used to verify top-1 correctness but operates
 * on any finding, enabling per-confidence-band accuracy tracking.
 */
function inferFindingCategory(finding) {
  if (!finding) return 'none';

  const id = String(finding.id ?? '');
  if (id.startsWith('finding:hypothesis:')) {
    return id.split(':')[2] ?? 'unknown';
  }

  const lower = `${finding.title ?? ''} ${finding.reasoningSummary ?? ''}`.toLowerCase();
  if (lower.includes('dns') || lower.includes('name resolution') || lower.includes('nslookup')) {
    return 'dns-failure';
  }
  if (lower.includes('secret') && (lower.includes('missing') || lower.includes('not found'))) {
    return 'missing-secret';
  }
  if (lower.includes('image') && (lower.includes('pull') || lower.includes('err'))) {
    return 'image-pull-failure';
  }
  if (lower.includes('volume') || lower.includes('pvc') || lower.includes('mount')) {
    return 'volume-mount-failure';
  }
  if (lower.includes('connect')) {
    return 'connectivity-failure';
  }
  return 'non-hypothesis';
}

/**
 * Infers the top category from a list of findings (uses the first / highest
 * confidence finding).  Used for the per-scenario `correct_top1` check.
 */
function inferTopCategory(findings) {
  return inferFindingCategory(findings[0]);
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Confidence bands
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Confidence bands used for calibration analysis.
 *
 * Findings are assigned to the highest band whose `min` threshold they meet
 * (exclusive upper bound, inclusive lower bound, except the top band which
 * is inclusive on both ends).
 */
const CONFIDENCE_BANDS = [
  { label: '0.85–1.00', min: 0.85, max: 1.01 },
  { label: '0.70–0.85', min: 0.70, max: 0.85 },
  { label: '0.50–0.70', min: 0.50, max: 0.70 },
  { label: '0.00–0.50', min: 0.00, max: 0.50 },
];

/**
 * Assigns a finding's confidence value to a confidence band label.
 */
function confidenceBand(confidence) {
  for (const band of CONFIDENCE_BANDS) {
    if (confidence >= band.min && confidence < band.max) {
      return band.label;
    }
  }
  return '0.00–0.50';
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Helpers
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

function parseJson(stdout) {
  const trimmed = stdout.trim();
  if (trimmed.length === 0) {
    throw new Error('CLI produced empty stdout');
  }
  return JSON.parse(trimmed);
}

function formatMs(value) {
  return `${Math.round(value)}ms`;
}

function printTable(rows) {
  const headers = [
    'scenario',
    'mode',
    'elapsed',
    'top_category',
    'top_conf',
    'correct_top1',
    'total_findings',
    'hypothesis_findings',
    'ai_findings',
  ];

  const stringRows = rows.map((row) => [
    row.scenario,
    row.mode,
    formatMs(row.elapsedMs),
    row.topCategory,
    row.topConfidence.toFixed(2),
    row.correctTop1 ? 'yes' : 'no',
    String(row.totalFindings),
    String(row.hypothesisFindings),
    String(row.aiFindings),
  ]);

  const widths = headers.map((header, i) =>
    Math.max(header.length, ...stringRows.map((row) => row[i].length)),
  );

  const renderRow = (cells) =>
    cells.map((cell, i) => cell.padEnd(widths[i])).join('  ');

  console.log(renderRow(headers));
  console.log(widths.map((w) => '-'.repeat(w)).join('  '));
  for (const row of stringRows) {
    console.log(renderRow(row));
  }
}

/**
 * Prints the confidence calibration report.
 *
 * For each confidence band, shows the count of findings in that band, how
 * many were "correct" (matched the scenario's expected top category), and
 * the accuracy percentage.
 *
 * Also checks monotonicity: higher bands should have >= accuracy than lower
 * bands and prints a warning when this is violated.
 *
 * Primary calibration gate lives in the unit suite
 * (`src/investigators/__tests__/hypothesis-ranker.test.ts`). This benchmark is
 * a secondary corpus-level signal used to spot broader regressions and inspect
 * confidence-band behaviour on the fixture scenarios.
 *
 * @param {Array<{confidence: number, correct: boolean, scenario: string, mode: string}>} allFindingStats
 */
function printConfidenceCalibration(allFindingStats) {
  console.log('\n--- Confidence calibration by band ---\n');
  console.log(
    'Definition of "correct": a finding is correct when its inferred category\n' +
    'matches the scenario\'s expectedTopCategory.  With 4 scenarios this is a\n' +
    'small dataset — use the data to spot regressions, not absolute thresholds.\n',
  );

  const headers = ['band', 'findings', 'correct', 'accuracy'];
  const bandStats = CONFIDENCE_BANDS.map((band) => {
    const inBand = allFindingStats.filter(
      (s) => s.confidence >= band.min && s.confidence < band.max,
    );
    const correct = inBand.filter((s) => s.correct).length;
    const accuracy = inBand.length > 0 ? correct / inBand.length : null;
    return {
      label: band.label,
      total: inBand.length,
      correct,
      accuracy,
    };
  });

  const stringRows = bandStats.map((s) => [
    s.label,
    String(s.total),
    String(s.correct),
    s.accuracy === null ? 'n/a (no data)' : `${(s.accuracy * 100).toFixed(1)}%`,
  ]);

  const widths = headers.map((h, i) =>
    Math.max(h.length, ...stringRows.map((r) => r[i].length)),
  );
  const renderRow = (cells) =>
    cells.map((cell, i) => cell.padEnd(widths[i])).join('  ');

  console.log(renderRow(headers));
  console.log(widths.map((w) => '-'.repeat(w)).join('  '));
  for (const row of stringRows) {
    console.log(renderRow(row));
  }

#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * scripts/benchmark-retrieval.mjs
 *
 * Retrieval quality benchmark — regression gate for retrieval refactors.
 *
 * For each diagnostic scenario, runs the lexical and graph retrieval pipelines
 * against hand-crafted seed queries, compares the results against a ground-
 * truth relevance oracle, and reports precision@5, recall@5, and MRR.
 *
 * Usage (requires a prior build):
 *   npm run build
 *   node scripts/benchmark-retrieval.mjs
 *
 * Output: per-seed stats table + per-scenario summary + overall macro-averages.
 *
 * NOTE — this script imports from dist/index.mjs (the compiled bundle).
 *        Run `npm run build` before executing it.
 */

import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);
const repoRoot = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
const distEntry = path.join(repoRoot, 'dist', 'index.mjs');

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Guard: require a build
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

if (!fs.existsSync(distEntry)) {
  console.error(
    `Build output not found at dist/index.mjs.\nRun \`npm run build\` first.`,
  );
  process.exit(1);
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Dynamic imports from the compiled bundle
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

  buildChunkIndex,
  LexicalSearchIndex,
  computeRetrievalMetrics,
  aggregateRetrievalMetrics,
  buildResourceGraph,
  getRelatedChunks,
  parseResourceJson,
  parseEventsJson,
  parseLogFile,
} = await import(distEntry);

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Minimal BundleProfile required by buildChunkIndex.
 * None of the retrieval functions inspect profile fields beyond shape checks.
 */
function makeMinimalProfile() {
  const cats = {
    analyzer: 0,
    log: 0,
    'kubernetes-resource': 0,
    event: 0,
    'cluster-info': 0,
    'helm-release': 0,
    unknown: 0,
  };
  return {
    manifest: {
      bundlePath: '',
      extractedPath: '',
      files: [],
      totalSize: 0,
      extractedAt: new Date(),
    },
    analyzerCount: 0,
    logFileCount: 0,
    resourceCount: 0,
    eventCount: 0,
    namespaces: [],
    workloads: [],
    coverageGaps: [],
    artifactsByCategory: cats,
  };
}

/**
 * Reads and parses all resource JSON files in the given directory.
 * Returns a flat array of K8sResource objects.
 */
function loadResources(dirPath) {
  if (!fs.existsSync(dirPath)) return [];
  const files = fs.readdirSync(dirPath).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json'));
  const resources = [];
  for (const f of files) {
    try {
      const raw = fs.readFileSync(path.join(dirPath, f), 'utf-8');
      resources.push(...parseResourceJson(raw));
    } catch {
      // ignore parse errors for individual files
    }
  }
  return resources;
}

/**
 * Reads and parses all event JSON files in the given directory.
 */
function loadEvents(dirPath) {
  if (!fs.existsSync(dirPath)) return [];
  const files = fs.readdirSync(dirPath).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json'));
  const events = [];
  for (const f of files) {
    try {
      const raw = fs.readFileSync(path.join(dirPath, f), 'utf-8');
      events.push(...parseEventsJson(raw));
    } catch {
      // ignore parse errors
    }
  }
  return events;
}

/**
 * Recursively finds all .log files under a directory.
 */
function findLogFiles(dir) {
  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
  const result = [];
  for (const entry of fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
    const full = path.join(dir, entry.name);
    if (entry.isDirectory()) {
      result.push(...findLogFiles(full));
    } else if (entry.isFile() && entry.name.endsWith('.log')) {
      result.push(full);
    }
  }
  return result;
}

/** Formats a float as a right-aligned fixed-decimal string. */
function fmt(value, width = 6, decimals = 3) {
  return value.toFixed(decimals).padStart(width);
}

## 36. toolResult
import type { BundleProfile } from '../types/index.js';

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Types
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface TraceStep {
  name: string;
  startedAt: string;
  completedAt?: string;
  artifactsProcessed: number;
  errors?: string[];
}

export interface InvestigationTrace {
  bundlePath: string;
  startedAt: string;
  completedAt?: string;
  steps: TraceStep[];
  profile?: BundleProfile;
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Factory
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Creates a new investigation trace for the given bundle path.
 */
export function createTrace(bundlePath: string): InvestigationTrace {
  return {
    bundlePath,
    startedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    steps: [],
  };
}

/**
 * Records the start of a new pipeline step and returns its index.
 *
 * The returned index should be passed to {@link completeTraceStep} when the
 * step finishes.
 */
export function startTraceStep(trace: InvestigationTrace, name: string): number {
  const step: TraceStep = {
    name,
    startedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    artifactsProcessed: 0,
  };
  trace.steps.push(step);
  return trace.steps.length - 1;
}

/**
 * Marks a trace step as completed, recording the finish time, artifact count
 * and any errors encountered.
 */
export function completeTraceStep(
  trace: InvestigationTrace,
  stepIndex: number,
  artifactsProcessed: number,
  errors?: string[],
): void {
  const step = trace.steps[stepIndex];
  if (!step) return;

  step.completedAt = new Date().toISOString();
  step.artifactsProcessed = artifactsProcessed;
  if (errors && errors.length > 0) {
    step.errors = errors;
  }
}

/**
 * Marks the entire trace as completed.
 */
export function completeTrace(
  trace: InvestigationTrace,
  profile?: BundleProfile,
): void {
  trace.completedAt = new Date().toISOString();
  if (profile) {
    trace.profile = profile;
  }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Augmentation helpers
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

function augmentResourceChunk(chunk: Chunk, resource: K8sResource): IndexedChunk {
  const summary = summarizeResource(resource);
  const identifiers = identifiersFromResource(resource);
  const ns = resource.metadata.namespace;

  return {
    ...chunk,
    summary,
    identifiers,
    namespace: ns,
    artifactCategory: 'kubernetes-resource',
  };
}

function augmentEventChunk(chunk: Chunk, event: K8sEvent): IndexedChunk {
  const summary = summarizeEvent(event);
  const identifiers = identifiersFromEvent(event);
  const ns = event.involvedObject.namespace;

  return {
    ...chunk,
    summary,
    identifiers,
    namespace: ns,
    artifactCategory: 'event',
    timestamp: event.lastTimestamp ?? event.firstTimestamp,
  };
}

function augmentAnalyzerChunk(chunk: Chunk, result: AnalyzerResult): IndexedChunk {
  const summary = summarizeAnalyzer(result);
  const identifiers = identifiersFromAnalyzer(result);

  return {
    ...chunk,
    summary,
    identifiers,
    artifactCategory: 'analyzer',
  };
}

function augmentHelmReleaseChunk(release: HelmRelease, artifactId: string): IndexedChunk {
  const summary = summarizeHelmRelease(release);
  const identifiers = identifiersFromHelmRelease(release);
  const content = summary;
  const lines = content.split('\n');

  return {
    id: `helm-release:${release.namespace}:${release.name}`,
    artifactId,
    content,
    startLine: 1,
    endLine: lines.length,
    summary,
    identifiers,
    namespace: release.namespace,
    artifactCategory: 'helm-release',
    metadata: {
      path: artifactId,
      category: 'helm-release',
      name: release.name,
      namespace: release.namespace,
      chart: release.chart,
      chartVersion: release.chartVersion,
      status: release.status,
    },
  };
}

/**
 * Truncates `text` to at most `maxChars` characters, appending `"..."` when
 * truncated.  Returns the full text unchanged when it fits within the limit.
 */
function truncateContent(text: string, maxChars: number): string {
  if (text.length <= maxChars) return text;
  return text.slice(0, maxChars) + '...';
}

function augmentLogChunk(chunk: Chunk): IndexedChunk {
  const path = getChunkPath(chunk);
  const identifiers = identifiersFromLogContent(chunk.content, path);

  // Try to extract a timestamp from the first line of the chunk
  const firstLine = chunk.content.split('\n')[0] ?? '';
  const ts = extractTimestamps(firstLine);

  // Build a meaningful summary that includes actual log content so that
  // semantic embeddings can match on log content rather than on the bare
  // file-path description.  ~500 tokens ≈ 2 000 characters at 4 chars/token.
  const MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS = 2000;
  const prefix = `Log chunk from ${path} (lines ${chunk.startLine}–${chunk.endLine})`;

  const chunkType = isRecord(chunk.metadata)
    ? (chunk.metadata['chunkType'] as string | undefined)
    : undefined;

  let body: string;
  if (chunkType === 'error-cluster') {
    // For error-cluster chunks, prioritise error/fatal lines so they appear at
    // the start of the summary and are captured within any token truncation.
    const contentLines = chunk.content.split('\n');
    const errorLines = contentLines.filter((l) => /\b(ERROR|FATAL)\b/i.test(l));
    const otherLines = contentLines.filter((l) => !/\b(ERROR|FATAL)\b/i.test(l));
    // Put error lines first, then the rest, then truncate the whole thing.
    body = truncateContent([...errorLines, ...otherLines].join('\n'), MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS);
  } else {
    body = truncateContent(chunk.content, MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS);
  }

  const summary = body ? `${prefix}:\n${body}` : prefix;

  return {
    ...chunk,
    summary,
    identifiers,
    artifactCategory: 'log',
    timestamp: ts ?? undefined,
  };
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// buildChunkIndex
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Builds a flat array of {@link IndexedChunk}s from all artifacts in the
 * investigator context.
 *
 * Every chunk includes:
 * - Stable citation metadata (chunk id, file path, artifact type, line range)
 * - A human-readable summary for LLM context
 * - Deterministically extracted identifiers for lexical matching
 *
 * Chunking parameters:
 * - Log files: 50-line chunks with 10-line overlap
 * - K8s resources: one chunk per object (preserves full structure)
 * - Events: one chunk per event
 * - Analyzer results: one chunk per result
 */
export function buildChunkIndex(context: InvestigatorContext): IndexedChunk[] {
  const chunks: IndexedChunk[] = [];

  // ── K8s resources ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  for (const resource of context.resources) {
    const ns = resource.metadata.namespace ?? 'default';
    // Use the actual source file path when available (populated by the pipeline
    // from the bundle manifest). Fall back to a flat namespace JSON path that
    // mirrors real Troubleshoot bundle layout (e.g. cluster-resources/pod/default.json)
    // so that grounding checks and evidence paths stay consistent even when
    // resourceSourceFiles is not provided (tests, minimal contexts).
    const fallbackPath = `cluster-resources/${resource.kind.toLowerCase()}/${ns}.json`;
    const resourcePath = context.resourceSourceFiles?.get(resource) ?? fallbackPath;
    const baseChunk = chunkResource(resource, resourcePath);
    chunks.push(augmentResourceChunk(baseChunk, resource));
  }

  // ── Events ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  for (let i = 0; i < context.events.length; i++) {
    const event = context.events[i]!;
    const baseChunk = chunkEvent(event, i);
    chunks.push(augmentEventChunk(baseChunk, event));
  }

  // ── Log files ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  for (const logFile of context.logs) {
    const baseChunks = chunkLogFileErrorAware(logFile);
    for (const baseChunk of baseChunks) {
      chunks.push(augmentLogChunk(baseChunk));
    }
  }

  // ── Analyzer results ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  for (const result of context.analyzerResults) {
    const baseChunk = chunkAnalyzer(result);
    chunks.push(augmentAnalyzerChunk(baseChunk, result));
  }

  // ── Helm releases ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  if (context.helmReleases) {
    for (const release of context.helmReleases) {
      // Use a deterministic artifact path derived from namespace and name so

## 74. toolResult
* @param resource The parsed K8s resource.
 * @param path     The artifact path used as the chunk's `artifactId`.
 */
export function chunkResource(resource: K8sResource, path: string): Chunk {
  const rawContent = JSON.stringify(resource, null, 2);
  const content = summarizeResource(resource);
  const lines = content.split('\n');

  return {
    id: `${path}:${resource.metadata.name}:resource`,
    artifactId: path,
    content,
    startLine: 1,
    endLine: lines.length,
    metadata: {
      path,
      category: 'kubernetes-resource',
      lineRange: [1, lines.length],
      kind: resource.kind,
      name: resource.metadata.name,
      namespace: resource.metadata.namespace,
      rawContent,
    },
  };
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// chunkAnalyzer
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Creates a single {@link Chunk} for an analyzer result.
 *
 * Content is a human-readable summary: `[SEVERITY] Title\nMessage`.
 *
 * @param result The parsed analyzer result.
 */
export function chunkAnalyzer(result: AnalyzerResult): Chunk {
  const content = `[${result.severity.toUpperCase()}] ${result.title}\n${result.message}`;
  const lines = content.split('\n');

  return {
    id: `analyzer:${result.name}`,
    artifactId: result.name,
    content,
    startLine: 1,
    endLine: lines.length,
    metadata: {
      path: `analyzer/${result.name}`,
      category: 'analyzer',
      lineRange: [1, lines.length],
      severity: result.severity,
      name: result.name,
    },
  };
}

## 76. toolResult
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Golden Set Expectations
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
//
// Defines structural expectations for each scenario. These are NOT about
// exact LLM wording — they verify that the correct root cause appears, that
// evidence is cited from the right artifacts, and that no hallucinations
// appear.

import type { FindingSeverity } from '../../src/types/index.js';

export interface ScenarioExpectation {
  /** Human-readable scenario name. */
  name: string;
  /** Minimum number of findings the pipeline should produce. */
  minFindings: number;
  /** Top finding title must match one of these patterns. */
  topFindingMustMatch: RegExp[];
  /** Acceptable severities for the top finding. */
  topFindingSeverity: FindingSeverity[];
  /** Minimum confidence for the top finding. */
  topFindingMinConfidence: number;
  /**
   * Evidence paths across all findings must include at least one match
   * per pattern.
   */
  mustCiteEvidence: RegExp[];
  /**
   * No finding title or reasoning should match these patterns.
   * Catches hallucinations of wrong failure modes.
   */
  mustNotHallucinate: RegExp[];
  /**
   * Root cause (reasoning of top finding) must match at least one pattern.
   */
  rootCauseMustMatch: RegExp[];
  /**

}

Short version: **you’re largely past the “can it do the assignment?” stage**.  
From the hiring PDF + PRD v2, this repo already appears to cover most of the core product surface. The biggest gaps left are now **proof, polish, and reviewer confidence** more than missing core features.

## What looks covered already

Against the PDF / PRD, you seem to have:

- CLI-first local analysis
- AI-assisted path plus heuristic fallback
- Markdown / HTML / JSON output
- evidence-backed findings
- contradictions / confidence / missing-evidence handling
- traceability
- multiple seeded scenarios
- approach writeup in `MY_APPROACH_AND_THOUGHTS.md`

So the remaining gaps are mostly:

---

## Highest-priority gaps left

### 1. Missing the most obvious hiring deliverable: a demo video link
The PDF explicitly asks for:
- a ~2 minute video
- a runnable repo
- a 1-page approach doc

You have the approach doc and repo, but I don’t see a video link in the repo/docs/README.  
You do have `DEMO_SCRIPT.md`, which is great, but a reviewer shouldn’t have to infer the video exists.

**Why this matters:** this is the single most visible “is the submission complete?” gap.

**Fix:**
- put the video link near the top of `README.md`
- add a tiny “reviewer path” section:
  - 1. watch 2-min demo
  - 2. run one command
  - 3. inspect sample report

---

### 2. Eval scaffolding exists, but the eval corpus is still narrow
You already have good foundations:
- `test/golden/...`
- `scripts/benchmark-hypotheses.mjs`
- `scripts/benchmark-retrieval.mjs`
- `test/retrieval/...`
- `test/integration/__tests__/real-bundle-regression.test.ts`

But the proof set still looks relatively small:
- `test/golden/expectations.ts` covers **4 main scenarios**
- `real-bundle-regression.test.ts` uses **2 regression bundles**
- several newer capabilities seem tested mostly at unit level rather than scenario/e2e level

**Reviewer question you still need to answer better:**  
“Does this generalize beyond your hand-crafted fixtures?”

**Best next move:** build a more explicit eval matrix:
- scenario
- expected top diagnosis
- expected evidence
- expected spec recommendation
- expected confidence band
- heuristic result
- AI-cloud result
- AI-local result

If you do one thing next, do this.

---

### 3. AI layer is less reproducibly proven than the heuristic layer
The repo has strong heuristic/golden coverage, and I see cassette infrastructure in `src/ai/recorder.ts`, but I don’t see a checked-in replay corpus / stable AI eval dataset wired into CI.

So right now the likely impression is:

- heuristic mode: well tested
- AI mode: architecturally strong, but less regression-locked

**Why this matters:** the assignment explicitly says “uses AI to analyze it.”  
To impress, you want the AI path to feel measured, not just present.

**Next step:**
- record a small canonical AI cassette corpus for 5–10 seeds
- replay it in CI
- add AI-specific scorecards:
  - citation grounding rate
  - top-1 root-cause accuracy
  - unsupported-claim rate
  - contradiction coverage
  - retrieval hit rate before LLM

---

### 4. Observability is present, but still pretty coarse
`src/pipeline/trace.ts` is useful, but it only captures roughly:
- step names
- timestamps
- artifacts processed
- errors

That’s good traceability, but not strong **diagnostic observability**.

**What’s still missing if you want this to feel elite:**
- per-seed latency
- retrieval breakdown by channel
  - graph hits
  - BM25 hits
  - semantic hits
- prompt token estimate / budget actually used
- grounding pass stats
- why a finding was merged/suppressed/deduped
- confidence deltas before/after verifier
- AI model/backend used per finding

**Best next step:**
extend trace output to include:
- `seedDiagnostics[]`
- `retrievalStats`
- `mergeDecisions`
- `confidenceAudit`
- `llmCallSummary`

That would make debugging and demos much stronger.

---

### 5. Docs are slightly out of sync with reality
Big one: `docs/findings-and-improvements.md` still reads partly like an active gap doc even though several items are clearly now implemented.

That creates a reviewer risk:
- `README.md` says “Complete”
- roadmap doc still says various things are gaps / future work / phased work

Even when technically explainable, that inconsistency can reduce confidence.

**Fix:**
- split `docs/findings-and-improvements.md` into:
  - `docs/completed-improvements.md`
  - `docs/future-work.md`
- or add a very clear header:
  - “Historical roadmap; several items below are now resolved”
- add a PRD coverage table:
  - requirement → file/tests proving it

This is low effort, high presentation value.

---

### 6. Feature-to-scenario coverage is incomplete
You’ve implemented more than your golden/e2e corpus currently showcases.

Examples that appear under-demonstrated at scenario level:
- network policy investigation
- node taint scheduling blockers
- MemoryPressure / OOM correlation
- Helm release diagnostics
- service/endpoints empty-backend scenarios as first-class top-finding evals

Some of these have solid unit tests, but not the same end-to-end “this wins on a real bundle” proof.

**To impress:**  
add 2–3 more seeded bundles specifically for:
- NetworkPolicy egress block
- Node taint / unschedulable workload
- Helm release failed/pending-install

Then include them in:
- golden evals
- benchmark scripts
- demo appendix

---

### 7. No visible CI/workflow story in the repo
I don’t see `.github/workflows/...`.

For an interview repo, this matters more than usual because it signals engineering discipline fast.

**Ideal minimal CI:**
- lint
- typecheck
- unit
- golden eval
- retrieval benchmark threshold
- upload eval summary artifact

Even a single clean GitHub Actions workflow would materially improve reviewer confidence.

---

### 8. Real-world robustness testing still seems lighter than correctness testing
You have lots of correctness tests, but the next layer to impress would be:
- malformed bundle tests
- corrupt tarball tests
- giant log / large bundle stress tests
- memory ceiling tests
- partial collection tests
- timeout / degraded AI backend tests at scale

You already have some degradation tests, but if you want to move into “strong evals and thorough testing,” this is the obvious next frontier.

---

### 9. Privacy / sensitive-data assurance is not yet a showcased strength
The PRD emphasizes privacy-aware / local-first behavior. You do talk about that well, and spec recommendations mention redaction, but I don’t see a reviewer-facing proof story like:

- reports do not leak secret values
- prompts do not include raw secret payloads
- secret summaries are key-name only
- redaction-sensitive fixtures are tested end-to-end

This could become a very differentiating trust feature.

**Great next eval:**  
“sensitive output safety”
- no base64 secret blob in reports
- no secret value in evidence notes
- no prompt payload includes raw secret data when avoidable

---

## My actual recommendation order

If your goal is to **thoroughly impress**, I’d do this next:

### Tier 1 — reviewer/completeness
1. Add demo video link to README
2. Add a “requirements coverage” table
3. Clean up stale docs / roadmap drift
4. Add sample report artifacts in `docs/`

### Tier 2 — evals
5. Build a real eval scorecard across all scenarios
6. Add AI replay/cassette evals
7. Expand scenario corpus to include newer investigators
8. Gate key metrics in CI

### Tier 3 — observability
9. Enrich `trace.json` with seed/retrieval/confidence telemetry
10. Add a debug mode that emits per-finding provenance
11. Add cost/latency/model summaries for AI runs

### Tier 4 — robustness
12. Corrupt/partial/oversized bundle tests
13. Stress/perf benchmarks
14. Sensitive-data leakage tests

## If I were reviewing this repo, my main remaining questions would be

1. **How well does it generalize beyond the current fixtures?**
2. **How reproducible is the AI path?**
3. **Can I quickly see a polished demo without running everything?**
4. **Can I inspect why the system made a call when it’s wrong?**
5. **How safe is it with sensitive support-bundle data?**

If you answer those five cleanly, you move from “strong prototype” to **“this person thinks like a product + systems engineer.”**
